            Some(cpu) => cpu.as_usize() as isize,
            None => -1,
        };
        // Simulate a round-robin selection starting from the last chosen CPU.
        //
        // It still checks every CPU to find the one with the minimum load, but
        // avoids keeping selecting the same CPU when there are multiple equally
        // idle CPUs.
        let affinity_iter = affinity
            .iter()
            .filter(|&cpu| cpu.as_usize() as isize > last_chosen)
            .chain(
                affinity
                    .iter()
                    .filter(|&cpu| cpu.as_usize() as isize <= last_chosen),
            );
        for candidate in affinity_iter {
            let rq = self.rqs[candidate.as_usize()].lock();
            let (load, _) = rq.nr_queued_and_running();
            if load < minimum_load {
                minimum_load = load;
                selected = candidate;
            }
        }
        self.last_chosen_cpu.set_anyway(selected);
        selected
    }
}

impl PerCpuClassRqSet {
    fn pick_next_entity(&mut self) -> Option<SchedEntity> {
        (self.stop.pick_next())
            .or_else(|| self.real_time.pick_next())
            .or_else(|| self.fair.pick_next())
            .or_else(|| self.idle.pick_next())
            .and_then(|task| {
                let thread = task.as_thread()?.clone();
                Some((task, thread))
            })
    }

    fn enqueue_entity(&mut self, (task, thread): SchedEntity, flags: Option<EnqueueFlags>) {
        match thread.sched_attr().policy_kind() {
            SchedPolicyKind::Stop => self.stop.enqueue(task, flags),
            SchedPolicyKind::RealTime => self.real_time.enqueue(task, flags),
            SchedPolicyKind::Fair => self.fair.enqueue(task, flags),
            SchedPolicyKind::Idle => self.idle.enqueue(task, flags),
        }
    }

    fn nr_queued_and_running(&self) -> (u32, u32) {
        let queued = self.stop.len() + self.real_time.len() + self.fair.len() + self.idle.len();
        let running = usize::from(self.current.is_some());
        (queued as u32, running as u32)
    }
}

impl LocalRunQueue for PerCpuClassRqSet {
    fn current(&self) -> Option<&Arc<Task>> {
        self.current.as_ref().map(|((task, _), _)| task)
    }

    fn pick_next_current(&mut self) -> Option<&Arc<Task>> {
        self.pick_next_entity().and_then(|next| {
            // We guarantee that a task can appear at once in a `PerCpuClassRqSet`. So, the `next` cannot be the same
            // as the current task here.
            if let Some((old, _)) = self.current.replace((next, CurrentRuntime::new())) {
                self.enqueue_entity(old, None);
            }
            self.current.as_ref().map(|((task, _), _)| task)
        })
    }

    fn update_current(&mut self, flags: UpdateFlags) -> bool {
        if let Some(((_, cur), rt)) = &mut self.current {
            rt.update();
            let attr = &cur.sched_attr();

            let (current_expired, lookahead) = match attr.policy_kind() {
                SchedPolicyKind::Stop => (self.stop.update_current(rt, attr, flags), 0),
                SchedPolicyKind::RealTime => (self.real_time.update_current(rt, attr, flags), 1),
                SchedPolicyKind::Fair => (self.fair.update_current(rt, attr, flags), 2),
                SchedPolicyKind::Idle => (self.idle.update_current(rt, attr, flags), 3),
            };

            current_expired
                || (lookahead >= 1 && !self.stop.is_empty())
                || (lookahead >= 2 && !self.real_time.is_empty())
                || (lookahead >= 3 && !self.fair.is_empty())
        } else {
            true
        }
    }

    fn dequeue_current(&mut self) -> Option<Arc<Task>> {
        self.current.take().map(|((cur_task, _), _)| {
            cur_task.schedule_info().cpu.set_to_none();
            cur_task
        })
    }
}

impl SchedulerStats for ClassScheduler {
    fn nr_queued_and_running(&self) -> (u32, u32) {
        self.rqs.iter().fold((0, 0), |(queued, running), rq| {
            let (q, r) = rq.lock().nr_queued_and_running();
            (queued + q, running + r)
        })
    }
}

impl Default for ClassScheduler {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self::new()
    }
}

impl SchedClassRq for FairClassRq {
    fn enqueue(&mut self, entity: Arc<Task>, flags: Option<EnqueueFlags>) {
        let fair_attr = &entity.as_thread().unwrap().sched_attr().fair;
        let vruntime = match flags {
            Some(EnqueueFlags::Spawn) => self.min_vruntime + self.vtime_slice(),
            _ => self.min_vruntime,
        };
        let vruntime = fair_attr
            .vruntime
            .fetch_max(vruntime, Relaxed)
            .max(vruntime);

        self.total_weight += fair_attr.weight.load(Relaxed);
        self.entities.push(Reverse(FairQueueItem(entity, vruntime)));
    }

    fn len(&self) -> usize {
        self.entities.len()
    }

    fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
        self.entities.is_empty()
    }

    fn pick_next(&mut self) -> Option<Arc<Task>> {
        let Reverse(FairQueueItem(entity, _)) = self.entities.pop()?;

        let sched_attr = entity.as_thread().unwrap().sched_attr();
        self.total_weight -= sched_attr.fair.weight.load(Relaxed);

        Some(entity)
    }

    fn update_current(
        &mut self,
        rt: &CurrentRuntime,
        attr: &SchedAttr,
        flags: UpdateFlags,
    ) -> bool {
        match flags {
            UpdateFlags::Yield => true,
            UpdateFlags::Tick | UpdateFlags::Wait => {
                let (vruntime, weight) = attr.fair.update_vruntime(rt.delta);
                self.min_vruntime = match self.entities.peek() {
                    Some(Reverse(leftmost)) => vruntime.min(leftmost.key()),
                    None => vruntime,
                };

                rt.period_delta > self.time_slice(weight)
                    || vruntime > self.min_vruntime + self.vtime_slice()
            }
        }
    }
}

// SPDX-License-Identifier: MPL-2.0

use super::{
    sched_getattr::{access_sched_attr_with, read_linux_sched_attr_from_user},
    SyscallReturn,
};
use crate::{prelude::*, sched::SchedPolicy, thread::Tid};

pub fn sys_sched_setattr(
    tid: Tid,
    addr: Vaddr,
    flags: u32,
    ctx: &Context,
) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
    if flags != 0 {
        // TODO: support flags soch as `RESET_ON_FORK`.
        return Err(Error::with_message(Errno::EINVAL, "unsupported flags"));
    }

    let attr = read_linux_sched_attr_from_user(addr, ctx).map_err(|_| Error::new(Errno::EINVAL))?;
    let policy = SchedPolicy::try_from(attr)?;
    access_sched_attr_with(tid, ctx, |attr| {
        attr.set_policy(policy);
        Ok(())
    })?;

    Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(0))
}

// SPDX-License-Identifier: MPL-2.0

use super::{sched_getattr::access_sched_attr_with, SyscallReturn};
use crate::{prelude::*, sched::SchedPolicy, thread::Tid};

pub fn sys_sched_setparam(tid: Tid, addr: Vaddr, ctx: &Context) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
    let space = ctx.user_space();
    let prio: i32 = space
        .read_val(addr)
        .map_err(|_| Error::new(Errno::EINVAL))?;

    let update = |policy: &mut SchedPolicy| {
        match policy {
            SchedPolicy::RealTime { rt_prio, .. } => {
                *rt_prio = u8::try_from(prio)?
                    .try_into()
                    .map_err(|msg| Error::with_message(Errno::EINVAL, msg))?;
            }
            _ if prio != 0 => return Err(Error::with_message(Errno::EINVAL, "invalid priority")),
            _ => {}
        }
        Ok(())
    };
    access_sched_attr_with(tid, ctx, |attr| attr.update_policy(update))?;

    Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(0))
}

use core::sync::atomic::Ordering;

use super::SyscallReturn;
use crate::{
    prelude::*,
    process::ResourceType::RLIMIT_NICE,
    sched::Nice,
    syscall::get_priority::{get_processes, PriorityTarget},
};

pub fn sys_set_priority(which: i32, who: u32, prio: i32, ctx: &Context) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
    let prio_target = PriorityTarget::new(which, who, ctx)?;
    let new_nice: Nice = {
        let nice_raw = prio.clamp(
            Nice::MIN.value().get() as i32,
            Nice::MAX.value().get() as i32,
        ) as i8;
        nice_raw.try_into().unwrap()
    };

    debug!(
        "set_priority prio_target: {:?}, new_nice: {:?}",
        prio_target, new_nice
    );

    let processes = get_processes(prio_target)?;
    for process in processes.iter() {
        let rlimit = process.resource_limits();
        let limit = (rlimit.get_rlimit(RLIMIT_NICE).get_cur() as i8)
            .try_into()
            .map_err(|msg| Error::with_message(Errno::EINVAL, msg))?;

        if new_nice < limit {
            return_errno!(Errno::EACCES);
        }
        process.nice().store(new_nice, Ordering::Relaxed);
    }

    Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(0))
}

The accounting problem is confirmed: `total_weight` excludes the current task because `pick_next()` subtracts it before the task becomes current, but `update_current()` now adjusts `total_weight` for the current task’s weight change anyway. A single current task changing from nice `0` to nice `1` is enough to underflow the counter.

   290	    fn enqueue(&mut self, entity: Arc<Task>, flags: Option<EnqueueFlags>) {
   291	        let fair_attr = &entity.as_thread().unwrap().sched_attr().fair;
   292	        let vruntime = match flags {
   293	            Some(EnqueueFlags::Spawn) => self.min_vruntime + self.vtime_slice(),
   294	            _ => self.min_vruntime,
   295	        };
   296	        let (_old_weight, weight) = fair_attr.fetch_weight();

   299	            .vruntime
   300	            .fetch_max(vruntime, Ordering::Relaxed)
   301	            .max(vruntime);
   302

   304	        self.entities.push(Reverse(FairQueueItem(entity, vruntime)));
   305	    }
   306	
   307	    fn len(&self) -> usize {

   309	    }

   312	        self.entities.is_empty()
   313	    }
   314	
   315	    fn pick_next(&mut self) -> Option<Arc<Task>> {
   316	        let Reverse(FairQueueItem(entity, _)) = self.entities.pop()?;
   317	
   318	        let sched_attr = entity.as_thread().unwrap().sched_attr();
   319	        let (old_weight, _weight) = sched_attr.fair.fetch_weight();

   321	        //
   322	        // self.total_weight = self.total_weight + weight - old_weight;
   323	        // self.total_weight -= weight;
   324	        self.total_weight -= old_weight;
   325	
   326	        Some(entity)
   327	    }

   329	    fn update_current(
   330	        &mut self,
   331	        rt: &CurrentRuntime,
   332	        attr: &SchedAttr,
   333	        flags: UpdateFlags,
   334	    ) -> bool {
   335	        match flags {
   336	            UpdateFlags::Yield => true,
   337	            UpdateFlags::Tick | UpdateFlags::Wait => {
   338	                let (old_weight, weight) = attr.fair.fetch_weight();
   339	                if old_weight != weight {
   340	                    self.total_weight = self.total_weight + weight - old_weight;
   341	                }
   342	                let vruntime = attr.fair.update_vruntime(rt.delta, weight);
   343	                self.min_vruntime = match self.entities.peek() {
   344	                    Some(Reverse(leftmost)) => vruntime.min(leftmost.key()),
   345	                    None => vruntime,
   346	                };

#!/usr/bin/env bash

# SPDX-License-Identifier: MPL-2.0

#
# assemble_review.sh — deterministic merge of persona comment fragments into the review file body.
# No LLM judgement here:
# group by persona in a fixed order,
# sort by file then line within each group,
# drop exact duplicates *within a persona*,
# write the frontmatter,
# and leave a <!-- SUMMARY --> placeholder for the orchestrator's LLM summary step.
#
# Usage: assemble_review.sh [--overwrite] <meta-file> <frag-dir> <output-file>
#   meta-file: lines `mode=`, `base=` (diff) or `files=` (files), `head=`,
#              `branch=`, `date=`, `title=`
#   frag-dir:  one <persona>.json per persona, each a JSON array of comments:
#       {"file","line","grounding","severity","problem","fix","diff"}
#   --overwrite: replace <output-file> if it already exists (otherwise fail).
set -euo pipefail

overwrite=0
pos=()
for a in "$@"; do
    case "$a" in
        --overwrite) overwrite=1 ;;
        --*) echo "assemble_review.sh: unknown flag: $a" >&2; exit 2 ;;
        *) pos+=("$a") ;;
    esac
done
if [[ ${#pos[@]} -ne 3 ]]; then
    echo "usage: assemble_review.sh [--overwrite] <meta-file> <frag-dir> <output-file>" >&2
    exit 2
fi
meta="${pos[0]}"; fragdir="${pos[1]}"; out="${pos[2]}"

# Refuse to clobber an existing review unless --overwrite (design §3).
if [[ -e "$out" && $overwrite -eq 0 ]]; then
    echo "assemble_review.sh: refusing to overwrite existing $out (pass --overwrite)" >&2
    exit 1
fi

python3 - "$meta" "$fragdir" "$out" <<'PY'
import json, os, re, sys
meta_path, fragdir, out = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2], sys.argv[3]

# A guideline short-name is a lowercase kebab identifier (e.g. `lock-ordering`) and
# renders as code — a citable rule. A bug's grounding is a short plain-language
# description (e.g. "Off by one") and renders as prose, so it never reads as a rule name.
KEBAB = re.compile(r"[a-z0-9]+(-[a-z0-9]+)*")
def grounding_tag(g):
    return "`%s`" % g if KEBAB.fullmatch(g) else g

meta = {}
for line in open(meta_path):
    line = line.strip()
    if "=" in line:
        k, v = line.split("=", 1)
        meta[k] = v

# Fixed persona order -> review section title.
# "Correctness" is the Development persona's section (a bare "## Development" reads oddly; see the design doc).
ORDER = [("maintainability", "Maintainability"),
         ("development",      "Correctness"),
         ("security",         "Security"),
         ("hardware",         "Hardware"),
         ("documentation",    "Documentation")]

def load(persona):
    p = os.path.join(fragdir, persona + ".json")
    if not os.path.exists(p):
        return []          # persona not activated / did not run: legitimately absent
    # An EXISTING fragment that is unparseable or not a JSON array means a pass
    # produced broken output. Failing closed (rather than dropping it) is essential:
    # this skill is recall-first, so a silently-discarded fragment is a silent
    # recall hole the benchmark/orchestrator would never notice.
    try:
        data = json.load(open(p))
    except Exception as e:
        sys.stderr.write(f"assemble: FATAL: unparseable fragment {p}: {e}\n")
        sys.exit(2)
    if not isinstance(data, list):
        sys.stderr.write(f"assemble: FATAL: fragment {p} is not a JSON array of comments\n")
        sys.exit(2)
    return data

L = ["---",
     f"date: {meta.get('date','')}",
     f"mode: {meta.get('mode','diff')}"]
if meta.get("base"):
    L.append(f"base: {meta['base']}")
if meta.get("files"):
    L.append(f"files: {meta['files']}")
L += [f"head: {meta.get('head','')}",
      f"branch: {meta.get('branch','')}"]
if meta.get("title"):
    # json.dumps yields a valid YAML double-quoted scalar (escapes embedded quotes).
    L.append("title: " + json.dumps(meta["title"]))
L += ["---", "", "# Summary", "", "<!-- SUMMARY -->", ""]

counts = {}
for persona, title in ORDER:
    # Dedup is per-persona: identical comments from two different personas are both kept,
    # in their own sections (design §4).
    seen = set()
    uniq = []
    for c in load(persona):
        # "Exact" duplicate: identical in EVERY field (a normalized full-object key),
        # so two comments that differ in grounding, severity, or diff are both kept.
        # A partial key would collapse distinct findings — a recall hole.
        key = json.dumps(c, sort_keys=True)
        if key in seen:
            continue
        seen.add(key)
        uniq.append(c)
    counts[persona] = len(uniq)
    if not uniq:
        continue
    # Sort by file, then line, then a stable text key
    # so co-located comments have a deterministic order regardless of pass output order.
    uniq.sort(key=lambda c: (str(c.get("file", "")),
                             int(c.get("line", 0) or 0),
                             str(c.get("grounding", "")),
                             str(c.get("problem", ""))))
    L += [f"## {title}", ""]
    for c in uniq:
        loc = "`%s`" % c.get("file", "?")
        if c.get("line"):
            loc += " line %s" % c["line"]
        L += [f"### {loc}", ""]
        diff = c.get("diff")
        if diff:
            L.append("> ```diff")
            L += ["> " + dl for dl in str(diff).splitlines()]
            L += ["> ```", ""]
        L.append("%s (%s): %s" % (grounding_tag(c.get("grounding", "issue")),
                                  c.get("severity", "major"),
                                  (c.get("problem", "") or "").strip()))
        L += ["", "**Fix.** %s" % (c.get("fix", "") or "").strip(), ""]

open(out, "w").write("\n".join(L).rstrip() + "\n")
sys.stderr.write("assemble: " + ", ".join(f"{p}={counts[p]}" for p, _ in ORDER) + "\n")
PY

# Summary

<!-- SUMMARY -->

## Maintainability

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs` line 108

> ```diff
> /// To handle this problem, we use a `pending_weight` field to store the new weight.
> /// ...
> /// - If the weight does not need to be updated (i.e. `weight & IS_PENDING == 0`),
> ///   we simply return the weight.
> /// - If the weight needs to be updated (i.e. `weight & IS_PENDING != 0`), we try to
> ///   store the new weight into the `weight` field with `IS_PENDING` cleared via a
> ///   `compare_exchange_weak` loop
> ```

`no-impl-in-docs` (minor): `FairAttr`'s rustdoc now documents private representation details such as `pending_weight`, bit-packing inside `weight`, and the `compare_exchange_weak` loop. In generated docs, those details read like part of the API contract and can go stale when the implementation changes. The same section also names a nonexistent `IS_PENDING` flag.

**Fix.** Keep the `FairAttr` rustdoc behavior-oriented, and move the lock-free implementation rationale to a private `//` comment near `fetch_weight()`. If the flag remains documented, refer to the actual `HAS_PENDING` constant.

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs` line 155

> ```diff
> fn fetch_weight(&self) -> (u64, u64) {
>     let mut weight = self.weight.load(Ordering::Acquire);
>     ...
>     self.vruntime
>         .store(vruntime * old_weight / new_weight, Ordering::Relaxed);
> 
>     (old_weight, new_weight)
> }
> ```

`accurate-names` (major): `fetch_weight()` is named like an accessor, but it can publish a pending update, rewrite `weight`, and rescale `vruntime`. Callers have to read the body to know the call mutates scheduler state.

**Fix.** Rename `fetch_weight()` to a side-effecting verb such as `apply_pending_weight()` or `refresh_weight()`, and update the call sites accordingly.

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs` line 320

> ```diff
> let (old_weight, _weight) = sched_attr.fair.fetch_weight();
> // Equals to:
> //
> // self.total_weight = self.total_weight + weight - old_weight;
> // self.total_weight -= weight;
> self.total_weight -= old_weight;
> ```

`explain-why` (minor): The comment before `self.total_weight -= old_weight` restates an algebraic expansion and refers to `weight`, which is intentionally not bound at the call site as `_weight`. It does not explain why removing `old_weight` is the correct net effect after a pending weight update.

**Fix.** Replace it with a why-focused comment, for example:
```rust
// A pending weight change would first replace this queued contribution
// with the new weight, then dequeue the entity. The net effect is
// removing the old queued contribution.
```

## Correctness

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs` line 147

> ```diff
> self.pending_weight
>     .store(nice_to_weight(nice), Ordering::Relaxed);
> self.weight.fetch_or(HAS_PENDING, Ordering::Release);
> ```

`careful-atomics` (major): `pending_weight` and the `HAS_PENDING` bit in `weight` are correlated state, but they are updated with separate atomics. A concrete lost-update interleaving is: update `1` stores `P1` and sets `HAS_PENDING`; `fetch_weight()` reads `W|HAS_PENDING` and `P1`; update `2` stores `P2` while the bit is already set; then `fetch_weight()` successfully compares `W|HAS_PENDING` to `P1`, clears the bit, and the `P2` update is never applied.

**Fix.** Protect the weight state with one synchronization object, for example a `SpinLock` around `{ weight, pending_weight }`, or use a generation-based CAS protocol that prevents `fetch_weight()` from clearing `HAS_PENDING` after a newer `pending_weight` has been published.

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs` line 183

> ```diff
> let vruntime = self.vruntime.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
> self.vruntime
>     .store(vruntime * old_weight / new_weight, Ordering::Relaxed);
> ```

`checked-arithmetic` (major): `vruntime * old_weight / new_weight` can overflow before the division. For example, a long-running nice `-20` task has `old_weight == 88817`; once its `vruntime` exceeds `u64::MAX / 88817`, applying a pending weight change wraps in release builds or panics in debug builds.

**Fix.** Use checked or widened arithmetic for the reweighting, and handle overflow explicitly, for example by computing in `u128` and clamping or by using `checked_mul()` with a defined overflow path.

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs` line 319

> ```diff
> let Reverse(FairQueueItem(entity, _)) = self.entities.pop()?;
> 
> let sched_attr = entity.as_thread().unwrap().sched_attr();
> let (old_weight, _weight) = sched_attr.fair.fetch_weight();
> ```

Stale priority ordering (major): `pick_next()` selects the heap entry before applying `fetch_weight()`, but `fetch_weight()` may change the same `vruntime` value used as the heap key. If task `B` is popped with stale key `900` and its pending change raises its adjusted key above task `A` at key `1000`, this code still returns `B`, so the fair queue no longer picks the minimum `vruntime` task.

**Fix.** Apply pending weight changes before heap ordering is observed, or reinsert and retry when a popped queued entity's adjusted `vruntime` no longer matches its heap key. A robust fix is to update queued entities under the owning run queue lock and remove/reinsert them with the adjusted key.

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs` line 340

> ```diff
> let (old_weight, weight) = attr.fair.fetch_weight();
> if old_weight != weight {
>     self.total_weight = self.total_weight + weight - old_weight;
> }
> let vruntime = attr.fair.update_vruntime(rt.delta, weight);
> ```

Integer underflow (major): `FairClassRq::total_weight` excludes the current task because `pick_next()` subtracts the popped task before it becomes current. In `update_current()`, changing the current fair task from nice `0` (`1024`) to nice `1` (`819`) with no queued fair tasks computes `0 + 819 - 1024`, which panics in debug builds and wraps to a huge `u64` in release builds, corrupting later `time_slice()` calculations.

**Fix.** Do not adjust `self.total_weight` for the current task in `update_current()`; only use the new `weight` for the current task's `update_vruntime()` and `time_slice()` calculations.

# Summary

The change improves the fair scheduler's handling of nice/weight changes by deferring reweighting until the affected task is observed by the run queue. The main risk is that the new deferred protocol mutates scheduler accounting and heap-ordering state from call sites that previously only read a weight.

Top issues:

- Major: `update_current()` adjusts `total_weight` for the current task even though the current task is not included in `total_weight`, so a nice decrease can underflow the run-queue total.
- Major: `pending_weight` and the `HAS_PENDING` bit are correlated state spread across separate atomics, allowing a later policy update to be lost when the scheduler clears an older pending bit.
- Major: queued tasks are selected by a stale heap key before `fetch_weight()` may rescale their `vruntime`, so the fair queue can return a task that is no longer the minimum.
- Major: the new `vruntime * old_weight / new_weight` reweighting arithmetic can overflow before division.

Structurally, the code would be easier to audit if the side-effecting weight-refresh operation were named as a mutation and if the detailed lock-free representation notes moved from public rustdoc to implementation comments near the protocol.

## Maintainability

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs` line 108

> ```diff
> /// To handle this problem, we use a `pending_weight` field to store the new weight.
> /// ...
> /// - If the weight does not need to be updated (i.e. `weight & IS_PENDING == 0`),
> ///   we simply return the weight.
> /// - If the weight needs to be updated (i.e. `weight & IS_PENDING != 0`), we try to
> ///   store the new weight into the `weight` field with `IS_PENDING` cleared via a
> ///   `compare_exchange_weak` loop
> ```

`no-impl-in-docs` (minor): `FairAttr`'s rustdoc now documents private representation details such as `pending_weight`, bit-packing inside `weight`, and the `compare_exchange_weak` loop. In generated docs, those details read like part of the API contract and can go stale when the implementation changes. The same section also names a nonexistent `IS_PENDING` flag.

**Fix.** Keep the `FairAttr` rustdoc behavior-oriented, and move the lock-free implementation rationale to a private `//` comment near `fetch_weight()`. If the flag remains documented, refer to the actual `HAS_PENDING` constant.

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs` line 155

> ```diff
> fn fetch_weight(&self) -> (u64, u64) {
>     let mut weight = self.weight.load(Ordering::Acquire);
>     ...
>     self.vruntime
>         .store(vruntime * old_weight / new_weight, Ordering::Relaxed);
> 
>     (old_weight, new_weight)
> }
> ```

`accurate-names` (major): `fetch_weight()` is named like an accessor, but it can publish a pending update, rewrite `weight`, and rescale `vruntime`. Callers have to read the body to know the call mutates scheduler state.

**Fix.** Rename `fetch_weight()` to a side-effecting verb such as `apply_pending_weight()` or `refresh_weight()`, and update the call sites accordingly.

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs` line 320

> ```diff
> let (old_weight, _weight) = sched_attr.fair.fetch_weight();
> // Equals to:
> //
> // self.total_weight = self.total_weight + weight - old_weight;
> // self.total_weight -= weight;
> self.total_weight -= old_weight;
> ```

`explain-why` (minor): The comment before `self.total_weight -= old_weight` restates an algebraic expansion and refers to `weight`, which is intentionally not bound at the call site as `_weight`. It does not explain why removing `old_weight` is the correct net effect after a pending weight update.

**Fix.** Replace it with a why-focused comment, for example:
```rust
// A pending weight change would first replace this queued contribution
// with the new weight, then dequeue the entity. The net effect is
// removing the old queued contribution.
```

## Correctness

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs` line 147

> ```diff
> self.pending_weight
>     .store(nice_to_weight(nice), Ordering::Relaxed);
> self.weight.fetch_or(HAS_PENDING, Ordering::Release);
> ```

`careful-atomics` (major): `pending_weight` and the `HAS_PENDING` bit in `weight` are correlated state, but they are updated with separate atomics. A concrete lost-update interleaving is: update `1` stores `P1` and sets `HAS_PENDING`; `fetch_weight()` reads `W|HAS_PENDING` and `P1`; update `2` stores `P2` while the bit is already set; then `fetch_weight()` successfully compares `W|HAS_PENDING` to `P1`, clears the bit, and the `P2` update is never applied.

**Fix.** Protect the weight state with one synchronization object, for example a `SpinLock` around `{ weight, pending_weight }`, or use a generation-based CAS protocol that prevents `fetch_weight()` from clearing `HAS_PENDING` after a newer `pending_weight` has been published.

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs` line 183

> ```diff
> let vruntime = self.vruntime.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
> self.vruntime
>     .store(vruntime * old_weight / new_weight, Ordering::Relaxed);
> ```

`checked-arithmetic` (major): `vruntime * old_weight / new_weight` can overflow before the division. For example, a long-running nice `-20` task has `old_weight == 88817`; once its `vruntime` exceeds `u64::MAX / 88817`, applying a pending weight change wraps in release builds or panics in debug builds.

**Fix.** Use checked or widened arithmetic for the reweighting, and handle overflow explicitly, for example by computing in `u128` and clamping or by using `checked_mul()` with a defined overflow path.

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs` line 319

> ```diff
> let Reverse(FairQueueItem(entity, _)) = self.entities.pop()?;
> 
> let sched_attr = entity.as_thread().unwrap().sched_attr();
> let (old_weight, _weight) = sched_attr.fair.fetch_weight();
> ```

Stale priority ordering (major): `pick_next()` selects the heap entry before applying `fetch_weight()`, but `fetch_weight()` may change the same `vruntime` value used as the heap key. If task `B` is popped with stale key `900` and its pending change raises its adjusted key above task `A` at key `1000`, this code still returns `B`, so the fair queue no longer picks the minimum `vruntime` task.

**Fix.** Apply pending weight changes before heap ordering is observed, or reinsert and retry when a popped queued entity's adjusted `vruntime` no longer matches its heap key. A robust fix is to update queued entities under the owning run queue lock and remove/reinsert them with the adjusted key.

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs` line 340

> ```diff
> let (old_weight, weight) = attr.fair.fetch_weight();
> if old_weight != weight {
>     self.total_weight = self.total_weight + weight - old_weight;
> }
> let vruntime = attr.fair.update_vruntime(rt.delta, weight);
> ```

Integer underflow (major): `FairClassRq::total_weight` excludes the current task because `pick_next()` subtracts the popped task before it becomes current. In `update_current()`, changing the current fair task from nice `0` (`1024`) to nice `1` (`819`) with no queued fair tasks computes `0 + 819 - 1024`, which panics in debug builds and wraps to a huge `u64` in release builds, corrupting later `time_slice()` calculations.

**Fix.** Do not adjust `self.total_weight` for the current task in `update_current()`; only use the new `weight` for the current task's `update_vruntime()` and `time_slice()` calculations.

# Expected defects

1. location: kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs (persona: development, grounding: careful-atomics, severity: major)
   defect: The change implements weight updates with an ad-hoc lock-free scheme across two independent atomics (`weight` with a `HAS_PENDING` bit and `pending_weight`) plus a `compare_exchange_weak` loop.
